2026-10-23 - fix(net): per-connection tick-frame arenas — L6 of the capacity report AND a real wire corruption. encodeTickFrame's shared frameArena returned views valid only until the next encode, but the collect window RETAINS queued views until flush: every batched conn's tick frame aliased the LAST-encoded frame (wrong ackSeq/inputAck per recipient, wrong record sets when tiers diverge). Invisible in uniform combat (frames are nearly identical) but live on any divergent-content tick. (1) Render clock re-anchored on every snapshot push, injecting WAN jitter as position warps — now free-runs and slews corrections; adaptive delay target is itself EMA-smoothed (was recomputing per-packet and breathing 100-350ms). Window now sized to the physics (convergeBudget: gap/horizon+speed over ACCEL_CAP). (3) Seam split by direction: longitudinal offsets (invisible, along-path) absorb per-frame; only perpendicular drift steers. (4) Converge chase baseline: sparse wire records strip VEL so serverVelocity read ~0 -> chase collapsed to dist/horizon ~1.4m/s and trailed the path forever; now falls back to velocity estimated from consecutive sampled positions, and onUpdate pos-delta reseeds no longer stomp the steered velocity mid-episode. 2026-10-19 - fix(interp): the ghost treadmill — remote replicas could diverge 100-450m from their own wire truth for tens of seconds. The dual demo diff isolated it: client remote lanes moved smoothly but tracked NO server trajectory (ruling out stale-stream and aliasing); live probing the interp internals showed snapshots arriving healthy (nSnaps growing, freshest ~50-200ms) while the RENDERED position sat hundreds of meters away — the fault is in remoteInterp's DR/converge state machine, not the wire. Chain: far-tier sparse records thin a fast remote's update stream → silence past drThreshold engages dead reckoning → simStep free-runs physics seeded from stale wire vel (sparse records carry pos+vitals only) → the ghost flies off in a wrong direction → when truth resumes, convergeStep's physically-capped chase (80m/s², 0.35-0.45s windows) cannot close a >100m gap against a 60m/s remote — timer expires, interp re-arms it, repeat — a permanent treadmill. Fix: gap >40m in interp() is no longer treated as a steerable seam — the replica SNAPS to the sampled path (vel=serverVelocity, terrain-clamped). A lost replica IS a teleport; one frame of honesty beats seconds of ghost. Verified live: per-seat gap-to-newest collapsed 100-450m→0-21m within ~9s, and the post-fix dual diff shows per-seat means 8-22m (interp lag + sparse cost — honest) vs pre-fix 57-199m/p95-488m. _tools/e2e/_interpprobe.ts dumps live interp internals (sim/conv/pred timers, simVel, snapshot buffer, gap-to-newest). InputStream.reset() clears journal + change-only edge state at the disconnect boundary (inputSeq stays monotonic): journaled inputs minted in the dead room used to replay into the next room's corrections (up to 128 wrong-room inputs through movement.update, then rubber-band), and held-input changes made on a dead lane never recorded — the next sendMove could skip the correcting edge while sendPosition kept reporting pre-flap held bits (server shadow runs phantom input -> repeat corrections). Claim-ledger sweep moved off the perf-HUD callback: bandwidth() was the only driver and early-returns when the HUD is hidden, so pendingHits grew all session and 'unconfirmed' desync telemetry never fired in normal play; sendPosition now sweeps at ~1Hz. JSON tickUpdate count clamped to updates.length (a count>len frame threw on undefined.playerId and swallowed the rest of the frame). wireIds.add now drops the decode baseline on a re-seat without remove, client roster AND shared WireIdRoster.feed — masked deltas can't inherit the departed owner's retained fields. handleCorrection rejects malformed positions before the log line reads .x. wire.ts stale record-size comment fixed (i16 era 24B/9B -> real 30B/15B). inputStream.test.ts: 4 pins on the reset contract.
2026-10-19 - fix(netcode): torn v4 frames evict touched decode baselines instead of poisoning them. decodeTickFrame decodes masked records into their retained byId baseline objects in place — a frame dying mid-way (truncation/corruption on the unordered lanes) left half-applied state that every later delta inherited. Now the decoder tracks touched wireIds and a fail() path evicts them: the corrupt frame degrades to a lost frame, post-loss deltas drop via the no-baseline empty-id convention, and the next forced full (~750ms) reseats — same lifecycle as an honest drop. Baselines the frame never reached survive untouched. wire.test pins the lifecycle: torn frame evicts the mutated baseline, spares the unreached one, delta drops, full heals. Also pins two gotchas found writing it: scratch-encoded records alias enc's buffer (copy before the next encode) and v4Last is a per-slot ring (one basis per wireId, as production).

2026-10-18 - fix(netcode): tick intro gate — joins stop shedding frames into their own outbox. A fresh seat's socket carries gameState + rosters + wireIds (often >256KB) while the client's main thread is still loading the world; every tick frame queued behind that burst was delivered stale or shed at the 256KB cap, and its seq gaps polluted both loss telemetry and tickNack storms (each nack re-stamps room introSeq — growing the very burst it complains about). sendBinaryToPlayer now holds frames ('held' status, separate tickFramesHeld counter on /metrics) until the outbox first drains below 32KB; the gate re-arms on every register (reclaim bursts are the same shape), a drained seat keeps the normal 256KB shed cap, and tickNack from a still-gated client is ignored server-side (its decode basis reseeds on the intro fulls by design). tickTier.test covers hold→drain→shed→re-arm; focused cluster green.

T1 has no cubemap skybox - its sky is a 16-wedge dome (each wedge a 180-degree zenith->nadir strip) with the wedge->texture index in the mission's Sky object, so the dome is resampled into cube faces by direction vector (azimuth -> sector, elevation -> strip row). 4 of the 7 maps reference dome art (litesky/marsdaycloud) and got maps/t1/<map>_t1/sky/{px,nx,py,ny,pz,nz}.webp + spec sky; the other 3 (iceridge, snowblind, stonehenge) shipped a flat sky colour in T1 and keep the procedural sky. UT-Onslaught-style node lattice, fully procedural: map span splits NxN (3/4/5 by span, ~200-350m cells), opposite corners hold alpha/bravo cores, every other cell a neutral node. Rules: touch a LINKED neutral node to start a 10s build (autonomous once armed); nodes act only 4-adjacent to your territory - no severing, ownership is permanent until razed; enemy nodes die to 10s uncontested presence-raze or blast damage (blast-only, matching the objective-durability rule; building nodes lose build progress instead of hp); a dead core (20s raze or 4000 hp of blast) ends the match. 2026-10-15 - fix(tests): the two load-sensitive gate reds were timing holes in the tests, not in the product. (1) resultSigner's "identical event streams produce identical hashes" pinned NOTHING: feed() stamps each event from simNow() on the 50ms grid and mint() signed endedAtMs: Date.now(), so two mints a millisecond apart (a full-suite box under load) produced different bytes - the red was two mints racing the clock, not a hash bug. feed() and mint() now take the stamp (defaulting to the same clocks, so production callers are unchanged) and roomMatch mints the receipt with the room's OWN simNow() on the same grid the event hashes use - which is what the signer's header always claimed a verifier could replay. (2) netbot wsFight hung every assert on ONE landed hit: measured noise floor on an idle box is shots=2-4, hits=1, while fireIntents queue ~20 (the server's fire throttle leaks ~1 landed hit/run), so under suite load the first engagement outlived the 20s/38s budget and it failed on `shots > 0` - the bots never got to trade, no claim property was at fault. Budget now 30s/60s, the starvation proxy counts kills+deaths too, and the run prints a WSFIGHT-TEL line for triage. Verified: resultSigner 8/8 x5, wsFight 3/3 (telemetry shown), full bun suite 1385 pass / 0 fail / exit 0.
2026-10-15 - test(e2e): per-map weather/atmosphere/palette spec. check.sh's visual gate only ever shot ONE map (mapshot.ts defaults to escalade), so the new terrainPreset 3-6 shader branches, the `weather` block and the `atmosphere` multipliers were invisible to it - a GLSL typo in preset 6, a dropped snow kind or a room hop that inherits the last map's tint would all ship green. `_tools/e2e/weather.ts` (wired into e2e/all.ts) boots the CONTROL map - one with no weather/atmosphere block, so it defines the boot sky + default trio - then hops whiteout/caldera/hoodoo/lagoon and back, asserting the live terrainPreset uniform, the 4 sky uniforms against the spec's multipliers and each ambient layer's geometry.drawRange against the weather block (incl. that a dialed kind is actually VISIBLE and an off kind is not). The return leg is the regression that matters: a hop back must restore the default trio + boot sky. The spec pins `fps-autoq` off through a new launchClient({preload}) opt - the dynamic-quality ladder sheds the ambient layers under sustained low fps, so without it every weather assertion is frame-rate dependent. Layers are identified by their position-attribute count (220/60/120/260) since AmbientWeather owns them and the Points objects carry no kind tag. It also writes _tools/e2e/shots/weather_<map>.png - the numbers prove the wiring, only the eye proves it looks right.

2026-10-15 - feat: 4 new custom maps + per-map weather/sky plumbing. New terrain palettes 3=snow/4=volcanic/5=badlands/6=coast live in world/terrainPresets.ts, spliced into the terrain fragment shader as a template string (same shape as core/gradeGlsl.ts UNGRADE_GLSL) because the ~900-line sizeWatch ceiling had terrainShaders.ts at 845 - the extraction takes it to 729. New MapSpec fields: weather (per-kind particle intensity {dust,embers,ash,snow}; ABSENT = the old dust+embers+ash trio, so pre-weather maps render byte-identical) and atmosphere (rayleigh/mie/sunIntensity multipliers on the boot uniforms + absolute starIntensity), threaded spec -> Room/roomStructures/MessageHandler -> mapUpdate/gameState -> client MapSnapshot/world.ts. main.ts's three always-on particle layers become one AmbientWeather owner that composes per-map weather with the dynamic-quality switch; new AmbientParticles 'snow' kind + setIntensity; AtmosphericSky.setAtmosphere/resetAtmosphere (caches boot uniforms so a room hop can't inherit the last map's tint). New _tools/mapbake/genmap.js = superset of genfield.js: custom octaves/amps, domain warp, ridge, tileable sinusoidal tilt, multi-mesa/moat/crater/rimring, soft strata - every shaping op periodic so the clipmap's RepeatWrapping never seams. Maps: Whiteout (alpine ridge field + two cirques, ctf, snow), Caldera (shield volcano with a summit crater, ctf, embers+ash), Hoodoo (mesa tableland over a canyon sink, dm, dust), Lagoon (island massif in a sunk lagoon, coast preset, ctf). Terrain gen was REBUILT after measuring the shipped T2 ports with two new tools, _tools/mapbake/terrainstats.js and hillshade.js: the first cut was a smooth plain with a fine carpet (sd 7-18m, nearly all energy above 200m), which is exactly what reads as flat and monochrome. The new fields normalize to the full byte range (so `height` = byte/255 spans 0..1 and the palettes finally use their whole ramp) and use a 5-octave fBm/ridged spectrum whose scale profile matches katabatic (relief 150-200m, sd 22-40m, mean slope 27-34deg, 10-26% over 45deg vs katabatic's 29deg/24%). genmap.js also gained domain-warped features - bowls, additive plateaus (the first cut blended DOWN to a fixed value, so every 'mesa' rendered as a pit), and a sea sink. Verified with hillshade renders + regenerated lobby thumbnails. Fog follows the engine's melt trick (the one that makes the sand map seamless): a spec must NOT set `fog.horizon`, because syncFog sets the sky's horizon band to the SAME sun-tinted colour it copies into scene.fog.color and the terrain's fog uniform, so fully-fogged ground dissolves exactly into the sky. Overriding horizon (my first pass) puts a differently-coloured band right where the terrain meets the sky. Close-range surface detail is a band-limited detail fBm (terrainShaders.detailFbm): 5 octaves at a detuned lacunarity of 2.03, each with a domain rotation + offset so the layers never line up - a single small noise is just one repeating tile - and each octave is faded out once its wavelength drops below the pixel footprint, so the layer is detailed at every scale and can never shimmer at distance.

2026-10-11 - renderer: WebGL context-loss recovery — previously an
unhandled loss left a dead page, and a page that lost contexts ended
Chrome-blocked from creating new ones ("web page caused context loss
and was blocked"). Now: webglcontextlost reloads the page (all GPU
state dies with the context — the standard three.js recovery), and a
construction failure reloads once after 1s for transient GPU resets.

    the interval itself (tickMs), so cadence and divisors can't drift.

    real clients on the same box were culled as dead sockets and froze

    'never reported'.

2026-10-03 - Sheep meter for server bots: pedestrian play (walking or
    parked) fills a 0-100 meter that drains while the bot covers ground,
    is airborne, or is jetting. At 100 the bot gets yeeted â€” a random
    impulse launch plus pinned jets, so the penalty for sheepish play
    IS the cure. Published on tickUpdate as a new trailing byte (0 for
    humans), rendered as a % badge over the bot's head and a SHEEP

2026-10-06 - demo format v6 (types.ts, demoWriter/Reader, FORMAT.md): two durability hardenings from the replay-format best-practice review. (a) projectileEvents gain a trailing u8 targetType — on Hit events it names the victim's lane (ball variant vs HIT_TARGET_NONE), closing the last raw-targetId lookup where a creature/deploy id aliasing a live ball could mis-score the frag; reader gates the 60-byte record on version, older files default 0 = ball lane which their data was anyway. (b) header gains tableHash = crc32 of the persisted enum tables (DEMO_SPECIES/DEPLOY/PICKUP kinds + ball variant arrays) stamped by the writer at serialize — a future table reorder or balance edit now flags old clips via a new demovalidate WARN channel instead of silently remapping ids. Writer stamps it live so a stale caller can't lie. Producer chain updated (recordHit takes the victim lane, impact.ts passes hitBall.variant). demovalidate also checks Fired shooter rosterIdx bounds and Hit lane membership. Verified: new v6 clip validates (formatVersion 6, tableHash stamped, 2815 checks 0 failures) and all existing v5 clips still pass (30k checks 0 failures) — backwards compat holds.
2026-10-06 - full demo audit round 2 — lane-namespace hardening (demo/types.ts, entities/targetIds.ts, server ballSpawn.ts): creatureDemoId masked netId with 0x7fff, so a creature netId over 0x3fff aliased the flag (0xc000) and pickup (0xe000) lanes every 16k spawns — cross-lane clobber in findAliveTargets, recoverMissingSpawns and the seek dead-ball set, which all keyed raw targetId across lanes. Those tables now key targetType*65536+targetId (demoClip.ts, playbackReconstruction.ts + playbackCreatures/Deployables/Targets consumers). creatureDemoId mask tightened to 0x3fff so creatures stay 0x8000-bfff forever; the ball allocator wraps below 0x4000 on both sides (client nextTargetId, server ballSpawn nextId — also stops server ids ever exceeding the u16 file field). Duplicate Spawned at a live ball id used to push a second Ball that never updated or disposed — orphaned frozen mesh; the spawn path now re-keys the existing instance. Deployable shots (weaponType 254, no ownerId) minted targetId=0 — same viewmodel-kick leak class as before; recordFired now attributes them to '[deployable]'.
2026-10-06 - playback audit now covers every lane (demoPlaybackAudit.test.ts, 16 tests): creature proxies must integrate ballistic drift (gravity species fall, mesh tracks pos); deployables spawn a scene mesh, dim on hp 0, pop+dispose on destroy; flags re-pin to the replayed carrier via remotePosAt; pickups spawn/remove; kill events emit a burst; remote replicas must interpolate forward on the real clip (a dead interp freezes all). scrubDemo() replays a whole file through the real lifecycle at 30fps collecting per-lane traces; a creature-bearing crossfire clip (midair_Cannon_4.60s_crossfire.demo, 27 creature + 109 flag + 34 pickup events) exercises the lanes henge lacks.
2026-10-06 - playback differential audit (demoPlaybackAudit.test.ts): file-level validation can never see playback-runtime bugs -- the ball freeze proved file-valid does not imply playback-valid. New vitest drives a real .demo through the actual createDemoLifecycle + applyPlaybackEvents path (real Ball/Projectile/terrain logic over stub scenery) and asserts the world animates: every spawned ball moves or provably rests on ground (never hovers), every fired projectile leaves its origin or terminates, only POV shots carry targetId 0, roster refs stay in bounds, local frames finite+monotonic. Includes the direct regression for the targetId-vs-instanceId freeze. Fixed the harness env en route: test setup's 2d canvas ctx stub lacked createRadialGradient (ball trail textures). Creature/deployable/marker lanes audited for the same key-mismatch pattern -- clean, they key by targetId consistently.
2026-10-06 - playback balls frozen midair (demoLifecycle.ts): updateBallsPlayback checked playbackBallById.has(b.id) -- but the map keys the DEMO's targetId while b.id is a fresh client nextTargetId(), so no playback ball ever updated; every spawned ball froze at its (often airborne) spawn point and read as a field of hovering nuggets. The update loop now iterates the map's own ball instances. Re-rendered clip: the midair ball field is gone, balls fall and bounce.
2026-10-06 - demorender arg parse footgun: render.js only accepts --fps=30 (equals form); '--fps 30' parses fps=true -> Number(true)=1, silently producing a 1fps file from a 10-frame loop. Rendered clips are now ffprobe-checked for literal fps, not just motion.
2026-10-06 - demo shot-attribution leak (demoEvents.ts + midairclip.ts): Fired events with missing ownerId (turret/deploy shells) minted targetId=0 -- the local-shot bit -- so deploy guns kicked the viewmodel while their bolts spawned across the map ('recoil animation but no projectiles'). Ownerless remote shots now adopt an '[unknown]' roster entry instead of 0. Second leak same shape: clip POV promotion re-stamped the shooter's shots to 0 but left the displaced observer's own local-lane shots at 0, phantom-kicking the swapped camera -- promotion now re-homes those to the observer's roster idx first. Verified on the air-to-air clip: only the shooter's 13 shots carry 0, positions trace his dive arc.
2026-10-06 - demo playback viewmodel fix (demoLifecycle.ts): frames'' weaponType was converted to a loadout SLOT index before vm.switchWeapon — but switchWeapon takes the wire weaponType. BoltLauncher/Disc worked by coincidence (wt==slot); every other gun looked up a wrong-or-missing model and rendered invisible hands (Cannon wt14 hit slot 10=Melee). Playback now passes the frame weaponType directly, unknown ids fall back to the stock launcher. Verified rendered: Cannon barrel shows bottom-right in the re-rendered clip.
2026-10-06 - bot head pitch: bots'' look was hard-zeroed every tick (rotation.pitch = 0 / moveState.pitch = 0), so every bot head faced dead level while their shots arced — demos and live matches read as horizontal-only aiming. Heads now track the live aim point: server bots read the held trigger''s aim via arsenal.aimPoint() (botDrive.ts), client bots remember the last fire command''s aim for 1.5s (botBrain.ts locomote), both slewed at the same 5 rad/s wrist turn as yaw and negated into the wire convention (positive pitch = looking down). Disengaged bots ease back to level. Verified on a recorded clip: 521/1872 remote frames carry nonzero pitch, spanning -1.40 (aiming ~80 deg up) to +0.85.
2026-10-06 - midair clip POV + vid analyzer: clip's local lane was whichever bot sat first in the player map, so rendered clips played through an arbitrary stationary bot while the real shooter never appeared as the camera. Fixed single-pass: the shooter-s remote samples get promoted to local frames at extract time (pos/vel/yaw at 20Hz camera track), his remote replicas drop out, and his Fired events re-stamp targetId=0 so the viewmodel kicks and wears his weapon. Added --minalt/--pick/--shooter/--norec selection flags; --norec prints the kill leaderboard and exits before buffers. New tools/vidcheck.py: ffmpeg-samples 1 frame/sec, scores mean per-pixel delta, verdicts FROZEN/SUSPICIOUS/OK — rendered clips are now machine-checked before sharing (broken spectator clip scored 0.05, fixed air-to-air 14.61).
2026-10-06 - midair clip harness (tools/midairclip.ts): offline 20-bot soak records through the real DemoRecorder at 143x wall speed, ledgers every midair kill (airtime/shooter/victim/terrain-clearance via botAttack+detonate patches and broadcast-intercepted kill stamps), then extractClip cuts shot-5s..kill+5s around the longest direct-hit. Fixes found by validating a rendered clip: kill stamps now carry _st kill-time (drain stamped batch-end, up to 100 sim-s late, sliding the clip window off the shot); blast attribution uses the same stamp (drain-time simNow compare never matched); local-bot shots were dropped entirely (now mint local-lane Fired events + serverId pairing); viewmodel weapon getter fed the bot's last-fired type; deadReckon off in harness (dedup ate every local frame across still-bot windows). Fired events now carry shooter rosterIdx+1 in targetId so playback kicks the viewmodel only for the local player's shots — every remote gun in a 10v10 clip was juddering the camera. render.js picks the largest live WebGL canvas (was grabbing a 300x150 dummy of 13).
